# Healthcare (Ritsuo Watanabe / Kei Takeuchi)

# Sector overall view for the next quarter

In medtech, stable end-demand should continue to support earnings, even amid a macro environment characterized by higher raw material costs and rising interest rates. Jan-Mar results confirmed solid performance at many major names, led by Terumo. If the market broadens after the AI-driven rally runs its course, we see a gradual correction of discounted valuations that have been overlooked. A resolution of medical device tariffs, scheduled for end-May, should also provide a tailwind. We believe this is a time to consider gradually adding risk exposure while monitoring the strength of the AI trade.

In healthcare services, developments following the June medical fee revision will be important. In particular, the pharmacy and hospice industries face a significant negative impact from the revision, and attention should be paid not only to the earnings impact but also to moves toward industry consolidation. For generics, the key issue is building production capacity to meet growing demand. Staffing services are showing signs of recovery, and we focus on the possibility that the busy Apr-Jun quarter could be evaluated positively. For healthcare IT, which we focus on, activity among hospitals and clinics remains sluggish, and we expect a relatively quiet quarter for earnings. In particular, M3 (2413) and JMDC (4483) have indicated low growth or profit decline for Apr-Jun due to reactionary declines from the previous year and upfront investment, and we see limited short-term investment appeal.

# Key catalysts / events

In medtech, the investigation considering additional tariffs under Section 232 of the US Trade Expansion Act, which began in September, reaches its deadline at end-May. We hope to see uncertainty resolved. In addition, June has a rich event schedule for individual names, including Fujifilm's CEO dinner on 3 June, Shimadzu's medium-term plan announcement on 4 June, Terumo's IR Day on 11 June, and Asahi Intecc's Thailand plant tour on 26 June.

In healthcare services, we do not see notable catalysts, but we focus on the possibility of MBOs and TOBs among small- and mid-cap stocks, as in 2025. Among individual names, Medipal HD (7459) is expected to complete the conversion of Paltac into a wholly owned subsidiary in early July, but it is unclear whether the earnings forecast revision will come at the 1Q results announcement or the 2Q results announcement.

# Recommended stocks

In medtech, we focus on Shimadzu (7701) as a name with company-specific catalysts, expecting an update on Tescan in the medium-term plan and greater clarity on capital policy. From the perspective of capturing the benefit of fund inflows into the overall sector, Terumo (4543) offers a high degree of comfort, as concerns such as one-off expenses and OrganOx are being resolved. Among small- and mid-cap stocks, we focus on Nakanishi (7716) and Menicon (7780), where the story of returning to a growth trajectory is attractive.

In healthcare services, we believe Medley (4480) in staffing services deserves the most attention, as we expect sales and profit to exceed guidance for the first time in three years. As investment targets with a high degree of comfort, we like generics names Towa (4553) and Sawai (4887). For Towa, this is driven by the contribution from the new product Efient AG (antithrombotic drug) and the production increase effect from the Yamagata plant. Sawai has been downgraded by the market owing to weak supply capacity, and we believe it is attractive from a valuation perspective.

BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

# Construction, real estate, and housing (Takumi Kasai)

# Sector outlook for the next quarter

Construction: In 1Q, updates regarding the impact of Middle East developments will be a key focus. While companies are expected to maintain their stance of passing on cost increases through price adjustments, risks remain that delays in the delivery of materials and equipment could lead to project completion delays and, consequently, postponed revenue recognition. Trends in order-time profitability should also be closely monitored. We expect operating profit to increase in 1Q for all companies except Taisei Corporation. Share buybacks are not anticipated.

Real Estate: The pace of office rent increases appears to have accelerated recently, and this trend is likely to continue into 1Q. We also expect progress in sales contracts for investment properties, as well as condominium pre-sales (with initial contract rates around 60–80%). As a near-term risk stemming from a deterioration in Middle East conditions, delays in material procurement could lead to postponed condominium handovers. In 1Q, in addition to the current procurement situation, it will be important to assess contingency measures in the event that such risks materialize. The pace of long-term interest rate increases also warrants close attention.

Housing: It appears that most companies have secured visibility on material procurement through June, suggesting that the impact of worsening Middle East conditions will be limited in 1Q. However, risks remain in terms of price increases and delivery delays for functional materials and housing equipment. Procurement conditions and pricing pass-through trends heading into 2Q and beyond should therefore be monitored closely. Interest rate trends in housing loans, both domestically and overseas, should also be watched as a risk factor.

# Catalysts and events

In the near term, the key events to watch are: (1) the BoJ Monetary Policy Meeting on 15–16 June, (2) updates on Middle East developments, and (3) Sekisui House's 1Q earnings announcement (4 June). Our economists expect a rate hike in June (0.75% → 1.0%). However, given that real estate stocks have already corrected significantly from their peaks, any additional negative reaction is likely to be limited. If Middle East tensions ease, we expect a positive share-price reaction for both construction and real estate sectors; in the short term, the impact is likely to be stronger for construction than real estate, as earnings concerns recede. On the other hand, if expectations for further rate increases decline significantly, real estate is likely to outperform. No positive catalyst is anticipated from Sekisui House's 1Q results, and attention will instead focus on housing material conditions and the latest trends in the US spring selling season.

# Recommended stock

We recommend Katitas (8919). The company's business environment—focused on purchasing and reselling existing detached homes, primarily in regional areas—remains favorable. With reduced supply of new housing and rising prices, the value of existing homes is being reassessed, and this trend is likely to persist for the foreseeable future. While the company's guidance does not incorporate the impact of Middle East developments, it appears capable of mitigating potential delays in housing equipment procurement through measures such as continued use of existing equipment or post-handover add-ons and additional work. Its high growth profile, with an expected operating profit CAGR of approximately $13\%$ through FY3/28 (BofA estimates), is likely to be re-evaluated in the current uncertain environment.
