Source: BofA Global Research   
Note: Share prices are as of 26 May 2026 close

BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH

# Price objective basis & risk

# Trial Holdings (141A / XIKRF)

We set our Price Objective (PO) at ¥5,400, based on c.9.5x FY6/28 EV/EBITDA. Given the company is viewed/valued as a discounter in its home market of Kyushu, we benchmark our valuation to three comparable peers whose EV/EBITDA multiples are around c.9.5x, and set our PO accordingly.

Upside risks are (1) successful establishment of the new store format, (2) retail-tech initiatives providing further differentiation, and (3) online grocery/net supermarket emerging as a meaningful growth driver. Downside risks are (1) a slower-than-expected recovery at Seiyu, (2) intensifying competition, and (3) external factors such as weather affecting near-term earnings.

# Sekisui House (1928 / SKHSF)

We base our price objective of ¥3,800 on our FY1/27 EPS estimate of ¥344.8 and a multiple of 11x, the average since 2020, when the company's US expansion gained momentum.

Upside risks are (1) share buybacks, (2) M&A, and (3) growth in energy-efficient housing demand.

Downside risks are (1) weak housing markets, (2) worsening real estate transaction markets, (3) further sharp increases in material prices, and (4) a rapid rise in interest rates.

# M3 (2413 / MTHRF)

Our ¥2,200 price objective is derived from our DCF model based on 20 years of earnings forecasts for coverage companies, although we also take into consideration other valuation metrics such as P/E and EV/EBITDA. DCF assumptions are a risk-free rate of

2.0%, an equity premium of 5.5%, and a perpetual growth rate of 2.0%. FCF risk is reflected in the equity beta, which considers quantitative risk derived from scenario analysis and qualitative risk based on factors such as management strategies. The equity beta is set at a tiered level within the sector. We base our price objective on a stock beta of 0.96, based on its two-year adjusted Bloomberg data, and WACC of 6.9%.

Our price objective equates to a P/E of 23x our FY3/28 EPS. Upside risk to our PO would be that revenues generated by new businesses leveraging M3's platform exceeding expectations while downside risks are (1) sustainability of growth coming into question, (2) growth investment expanding substantially above expectations, and (3) risk tolerance for stocks from other sectors increasing.

# Kirin Holdings (2503 / KNBWF)

Our price objective of ¥2,800 is based on an EV/EBITDA multiple of 11.8x for FY12/26 EBITDA excluding Kyowa Kirin (three-year historical average: 11.3x). We estimate that Kirin's enterprise value reflects a discount versus its theoretical shareholder value on a sum-of-the-parts basis, with the discount corresponding to the unrealized gains associated with Kyowa Kirin.

Upside risks to our price objective are (1) stronger-than-expected synergies in the Health Science business, (2) progress in portfolio divestitures or reorganization, (3) better-than-expected profitability improvement at Kirin Brewery, and (4) faster profit growth at Kyowa Kirin.

Downside risks are (1) impairment losses related to acquired businesses, (2) intensifying competitive environments across key markets, and (3) accelerated tightening of global alcohol regulations.

# Kikkoman (2801 / KIKOF)

Our price objective of ¥1,800 is based on applying a P/E of 24x to our FY2026 forecast (ending in March 2027), which is one standard deviation below the historical average. This corresponds to a P/B of 3.1x and an EV/EBITDA multiple of 14.0x on FY2026 estimates. Downside risks to our price objective are: (1) slower sales growth in North America and Europe, (2) profit erosion from yen appreciation on currency translation, and (3) limited progress on capital efficiency improvement measures. Upside risks are: (1) an acceleration in North America sales growth, (2) potential policy changes in the US such as tax cuts or demand stimulus measures, (3) increased relative attractiveness driven by yen depreciation, and (4) a higher likelihood of achieving the ROE target ahead of schedule.

# Kobe Bussan (3038 / KOBNF)

We set our Price Objective at ¥4,150, corresponding to a P/E of approximately 27x our FY10/27 estimates. As a reference point, during Oct 2025 to March 2026, when the sales momentum slowed down, P/E traded around 27x, we used this trading range to inform the PO setting. Upside risks would be an acceleration in store openings and yen appreciation that would lift margins, while downside risks are a sales slowdown that could prompt a short term share price correction and sharp yen depreciation that would raise procurement costs beyond the company's ability to absorb in the near term.

# NEXON (3659 / NEXOF)

Our PO of ¥2,270 is based on applying approximately 18x P/E to our FY12/26E EPS of ¥123. This represents an approximately 20% discount to the FY26 average P/E of approximately 23x for the 10 companies in the Game & Content cluster we cover. We apply this discount as we expect the company's EPS CAGR for FY12/27-FY12/28 to be approximately 2%, below the cluster average of approximately 12%.

Upside risks to our PO are: new hit titles, success in Western markets, and progress in shareholder returns. Downside risks are: weakness in the PC game business in China and Korea, tighter game regulations in both markets, and higher share price volatility due to quarterly guidance updates.

# Tokyo Ohka Kogyo (4186 / TOKCF)

Our price objective of ¥14,300 is derived by applying a 37x P/E multiple to our FY12/27 EPS forecast.

The 37x multiple represents the upper bound of the past three-year average plus 1σ level, and reflects the company's deep involvement in technological innovation across both front-end and back-end semiconductor processes, particularly in advanced applications such as generative AI.

Downside risks are 1) longer than expected cooling demand in the semiconductor market, 2) customers failing to adopt Tokyo Ohka Kogyo's products for use in production lines for cutting-edge devices, 3) trend change of semiconductor market caused by US-China trade friction, and 4) sharp yen appreciation (FX sensitivity of OP: ¥0.2bn).

# NOM Institute (4307 / NURAF / NRILY)

We derive our ¥6,100 price objective (ADR: US\$39.35) by applying a FY3/28E P/E of 25x to our FY3/28 EPS forecast of ¥244.4. The 25x multiple corresponds to the three-year average forward P/E minus 1σ, and compares with the past five-year forward P/E range of 18-39x. While we expect earnings to remain solid, this implies a historically low valuation. However, in light of recent market dynamics, including the "Anthropic shock," we view this level as appropriate.

Upside risks to our price objective are: (1) stronger-than-expected domestic order growth, (2) greater-than-expected improvement in gross margins, (3) better visibility on the overseas business outlook, and (4) additional share buybacks.

Downside risks are: (1) weaker domestic orders, and (2) supply-demand deterioration due to share sales by the largest shareholder.

# Fuso Chemical (4368 / XQRDF)

Our ¥5,000 price objective is based on applying a 30.0x P/E multiple to our FY3/27E EPS. Ultra high purity colloidal silica has already driven full utilization of production lines, supported solely by demand from generative AI and other advanced logic applications. More recently, China and back end advanced packaging demand have emerged as additional growth drivers. Given that growth expectations for the company are now at their highest level in years, we believe it is appropriate to apply a valuation of +1σ to the company's post 2020 peak multiple.

Upside risks are 1) Colloidal silica price hikes exceeding depreciation burden、2) Colloidal silica price hikes exceeding depreciation burden、3) Colloidal silica price hikes exceeding depreciation burden 4) Stronger malic acid export growth to Europe/US. Downside risks are、1) Sharp semiconductor market downturn、2) Delays in major semiconductor fab construction、3) Weak malic acid demand、4) Greater competitive entry into colloidal silica

# Kao (4452 / KAO CF)

Our PO of ¥6,400 is based on P/B-ROE correlation, and derived by applying a P/B multiple of 2.6x (based on our FY26/12E ROE) and multiplying it by our FY26/12E BPS. Upside risks are earnings improvement driven by a sharp recovery in the cosmetics business, the announcement of M&A, and early disclosure of share buybacks. Downside risks are persistently high crude oil prices, supply constraints stemming from difficulties in procuring naphtha, and a further deterioration in Japan-China relations leading to weaker inbound demand, among others.

# Medley (4480 / XEQNF)

Our ¥3,200 price objective is calculated based on the SOTP (sum-of-the-parts) method. A DCF model was used to derive the valuation for the Recruitment PF, which has a high visibility for FCF, and consolidated costs, based on a 20-year earnings forecast. Our

primary DCF assumptions are a risk-free rate of 2.0%, risk premium of 5.5%, WACC of 7.7%, and equity beta of 1.25. The beta is based on an assessment of the degree of risk to future earnings using Bloomberg's two-year adjusted beta. Based on the above, value per share is ¥1,410. EV/sales multiple is the base to assess the Medical PF business, which has a high growth potential, and new businesses. Value per share is ¥1,820, which is 3.5x FY12/27 segment sales forecast. Upside risk to our price objective would be new businesses that leverage the Recruitment PF exceeding expectations. Downside risks are 1) a lack of progress in the government's promotion of healthcare digitalization or failure of Medley to substantially expand its online medication service due to intense competition, 2) pricing erosion in the Recruitment PF that depresses profitability, and 3) insufficient solutions tools for the Medical PF.

# JMDC (4483 / JMDCF)

Our ¥4,100 price objective is derived from our DCF model based on 20 years of earnings forecasts for coverage companies, taking into consideration other valuation metrics such as EV/EBITDA. DCF assumptions are a risk-free rate of 2.0%, an equity premium of 5.5%, and a perpetual growth rate of 2.0%. FCF risk is reflected in the equity beta, which considers quantitative risk derived from scenario analysis and qualitative risk based on factors such as management strategies. The equity beta of 1.05 is based on two-year adjusted Bloomberg data, and WACC of 6.9%. Our price objective corresponds to a P/E of 36x and EV/EBITDA of 18x FY3/27 forecasts. Downside risks to our price objective are a lack of progress in government policies to advance healthcare digitalization, utilization of healthcare big data falling short of our projections, inability to secure adequate human resources to support its data analysis and processing technology, and equity dilution from issuance of new shares to meet increased investment needs.

# Takeda Pharm. (4502 / TKPHF / TAK)

We base our JPY6,000 PO (ADR: \$19.40) on our 12-year DCF model and also taking into account valuations such as P/E and P/B. Our DCF assumptions are risk-free rate 1.5%, equity risk premium 5.5%, terminal growth 1.0% and tax rate 40%. Individual company FCF risk is reflected in the stock beta, which takes into account quantitative risk based on new drug development scenario analysis and qualitative risk based on factors such as management strategies, and is set at a tiered level within the sector. We use the Bloomberg two-year adjusted beta of 0.83, and base our price objective for the company on a stock beta of 0.91, and WACC of 4.8%. Our PO equates to a P/E of 13x our FY3/28 Core EPS forecast. Upside risks are favorable new drug pipeline data and better-than-expected progress in cost reductions. Downside risks are a cancellation or postponement of new product development or changes in product evaluation, inaccuracy in our DCF assumptions and higher-than-expected impairment losses on intangible assets.

# Astellas Pharma (4503 / ALPMF / ALPMY)

We base our JPY2,950 PO (ADR: \$19.00) on our 12-year DCF model and also taking into account valuations such as P/E and P/B. Our DCF assumptions are risk-free rate 1.5%, equity risk premium 5.5%, terminal growth 1.0% and tax rate 40%. Individual company FCF risk is reflected in the stock beta, which takes into account quantitative risk based on new drug development scenario analysis and qualitative risk based on factors such as management strategies, and is set at a tiered level within the sector. The equity beta used in our price objective calculation is 0.87, based on Bloomberg's two-year adjusted beta, and the WACC is 5.6%. Our PO equates to a EV/EBITDA of 9x on our FY3/28 estimates. Downside risks to our price objective are lower-than-expected core product sales, cancellation or postponement of new product development or changes in product evaluation, and a divergence between actual conditions and those assumed in our DCF calculations.

# Eisai (4523 / ESALF)

We base our ¥4,000 price objective on our DCF model, while also taking into account valuations such as P/E and P/B. Our DCF assumptions are risk-free rate 1.5%, equity risk premium 5.5%, terminal growth 1% and tax rate 40%. Individual company FCF risk is reflected in the stock beta, which takes into account quantitative risk based on new drug development scenario analysis and qualitative risk based on factors such as management strategies, and it is set at a tiered level within the sector. The equity beta used in our price objective calculation is 0.87, based on Bloomberg's two-year adjusted beta, and the WACC is 5.9%. Our PO corresponds to a P/E of 18x our FY3/27 EPS forecast. Downside risks are (1) mainstay drugs missing company guidance, (2) cancellation or postponement of new product development or changes in product evaluation, (3) adverse decisions by regulatory authorities in other countries and harsh stances on coverage by public health insurance, and (4) divergence between actual conditions and those assumed in our DCF calculations. Upside risks are greater-than-expected sales of Leqembi and development successes that generate data differentiating new drug candidates.

# Terumo (4543 / TRUMF)

We base our ¥2,900 PO on EV/EBITDA of 14.0x on our FY3/27 estimates. We believe this is achievable as Terumo operates in multiple business areas, which leads to stable earnings. Downside risks to our PO are sales growth of highly anticipated WEB falling short of expectations, profit margins being squeezed by growth investment, deceleration of businesses acquired through M&A, and product acquisitions and other such activities stalling. Upside risks are higher-than-expected premium valuation in view of high earnings stability, and higher-than-expected growth through M&A.

# Towa Pharmaceutical (4553 / TWAPF)

We set a PO of ¥5,300, derived primarily from a discounted cash flow (DCF) valuation based on a 20-year earnings forecast for the company, with P/E and P/B multiples used as cross-checks. Key assumptions in the DCF include a risk-free rate of 2.0%, an equity risk premium of 5.5%, a terminal growth rate of 2.0%, and a tax rate of 40%. Company-specific free cash flow risk is reflected in the equity beta, which is set in a stepwise manner based on quantitative risks derived from scenario analysis for new product development as well as qualitative risks related to management strategy.
The equity beta used in our PO calculation is 0.89, based on Bloomberg's two-year adjusted beta, resulting in a WACC of 4.3%. This corresponds to a P/E of 13x on our FY3/27E.

Downside risks to our PO are slower-than-expected expansion of the generic drug market, intensifying competition, profit pressure arising from drug pricing system reforms, deterioration in financial metrics due to expanded capital expenditure, and changes in the government's promotion policy for generics. Upside risks are stronger-than-expected penetration of generic drugs, easing competitive dynamics in the generic market, and further progress in discussions surrounding drug pricing system and policies to promote generic drug usage.

# Daiichi Sankyo (4568 / DSKYF / DSNKY)

We base our ¥4,500 PO (ADR: \$29.00) on our 12-year DCF model and also taking into account valuations such as P/E and P/B. Our DCF assumptions are risk-free rate 1.5%, equity risk premium 5.5%, terminal growth 1.0% and tax rate 40%. Individual company FCF risk is reflected in the stock beta, which takes into account quantitative risk based on new drug development scenario analysis and qualitative risk based on factors such as management strategies, and is set at a tiered level within the sector. The equity beta used in our price objective calculation is 0.93, based on Bloomberg's two-year adjusted beta, and the WACC is 6.5%. Our PO equates to a P/E of 25x our FY3/28 EPS forecast. Downside risks to our PO are the sudden emergence of adverse side effects, intensifying competition, declining profits as a result of increased pressure from healthcare policies, delays in or discontinuation of new drug development, development setbacks in clinical trials, and greater-than-expected costs (mainly SG&A expenses in the US), and changes to product assessment. Upside risks are greater-than-expected profits on easing of competition or new drug demand, new profit opportunities not currently discounted such as might result from M&A, and healthcare policies from different countries that have a positive impact on product sales. Upside risk related to pipeline is that P3 trials show a positive outcome.

# Otsuka HD (4578 / OTSKF / OTSKY)

We base our ¥12,400 (ADR: \$40.00) price objective on SOTP analysis for the pharmaceutical segment, biopharma business, and the NC/consumer product business. For the biopharma business, we use a 12-year DCF model, and our DCF assumptions are risk-free rate 1.5%, equity risk premium 5.5%, terminal growth 1.0% and tax rate 40%. Individual company FCF risk is reflected in the stock beta, which takes into account quantitative risk based on new drug development scenario analysis and qualitative risk based on factors such as management strategies, and is set at a tiered level within the sector. The equity beta used in our price objective calculation is 0.87, based on Bloomberg's two-year adjusted beta, and the WACC is 6.1%. Our price objective corresponds to a EV/EBITDA of 9.5x our FY12/28 EPS forecast. Upside risks to our price objective are greater-than-expected sales of in-house drugs, increases in royalty income, and evidence that differentiates Otsuka drug candidates. Downside risks are weaker-than-anticipated sales of mainstay drugs, cancellation or postponement of new product development or changes in product evaluation, commercialization of novel competing drugs, and divergence between actual conditions and those assumed in our DCF calculations.

# Obic (4684 / OBIIF / OBICY)

We derive our ¥5,600 price objective (ADR: \$36.13) by applying a P/E of 27x to our FY3/28 EPS forecast of ¥208.9. The 27x multiple corresponds to the three-year average forward P/E minus one standard deviation (with the five-year forward P/E range at 21-47x). While this represents a relatively low level versus historical valuations despite solid earnings performance, we view this as appropriate in light of recent equity market conditions.

Upside risks to our price objective are: (1) acceleration in revenue growth in system integration (SI), and (2) further expansion of shareholder returns.

Downside risks to our price objective are: (1) revenue declines in SI, and (2) a halt in gross margin improvement.

# Obic Business Consultants (4733 / OBIBF)

We derive our ¥8,800 price objective by applying a P/E of 30x to our FY3/28 EPS forecast of ¥294. 30x multiple is close to the three-year average forward P/E minus one standard deviation (with the five-year forward P/E range at 23-42x). While the applied P/E may appear elevated on a relative basis, adjusting for the company's projected excess cash for FY3/28 (equivalent to more than two months of sales) and investment securities implies an effective P/E of 20x. We therefore view the applied multiple as not demanding in real terms.

Upside risks to our price objective: (1) Higher than expected growth in core business cloud SaaS subscriptions, (2) ROE accretive shareholder returns, including a higher payout ratio or the introduction of a DOE framework, (3) potential full acquisition by OBIC, the largest shareholder.

Downside risks : (1) slower growth in core business cloud SaaS subscriptions, (2) intensifying competition.

# CyberAgent (4751 / CYAGF)

Our PO of ¥1,820 is based on applying a 19.5x multiple to our FY9/26E EPS of ¥93.2. The 19.5x multiple represents a 15% discount to the FY26 average PER of 23x for the 13 companies in the Game & Content cluster we cover. We apply this discount as we expect the company's EPS CAGR for FY9/27-FY9/28 to be approximately 9%, below the cluster average of approximately 12%.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) weaker-than-expected sales in the game business, (2) a slowdown in the advertising market, and (3) widening losses in the media business due to intensified competition.

# Sawai Group Holdings (4887 / SWGHF)

We calculate our JPY2,300 price objective using a 20-year DCF model, and taking into account valuations such as P/E and P/B. Our DCF assumptions are risk-free rate 2.0%, equity risk premium 5.5%, terminal growth 2.0% and tax rate 30%. Individual company FCF risk is reflected in the stock beta, which factors in quantitative risk based on new drug development scenario analysis and qualitative risk based on factors such as management strategies. We use an adjusted 2-year beta of 0.81 based on the data provided by Bloomberg, and WACC of 5.0%. Our PO equates to a P/E of 14x our FY3/27 EPS forecast. Downside risks to our price objective are delayed expansion of the generic drug market, intensifying competition, pressure on profits due to changes to the NHI price system, a worsening in the balance sheet stemming from expanded capital investment, and changes to government stimulatory policies. Upside risks are better-than-projected market penetration of generic drugs, an easing of competition in the generic drug market, and positive progress in government debates on the NHI price system and generic stimulus measures.

# Lion (4912 / LIOPF)

Our PO of ¥2,000 is based on the P/B-ROE correlation, derived by applying a P/B multiple of 1.9x (based on our FY12/26E ROE) and multiplying it by our FY12/26E BPS. Downside risks are persistently high crude oil prices, supply constraints due to difficulties in naphtha procurement, further intensification of competition in key emerging markets, and large scale M&A transactions that may be viewed as overvalued by the market.

# Toyo Tire (5105 / TOTTF)

Our PO for Toyo Tire is ¥4,100. P/E is 9x, the average for FY12/19-FY12/20, the last time share price increased on the back of WLTR sales recovery. We apply this P/E to the average of our EPS estimates for FY12/26 and FY12/27 of ¥464. Downside risks are escalating competition for offroad tires (for passenger vehicles and light trucks), where Toyo Tire currently has a commanding position, and lower prices for replacement tires when materials costs are falling. Early recovery in the North America sales environment is an upside risk.

# Sumitomo Rubber Industries (5110 / SMTUF)

Our PO for Sumitomo Rubber Industries is ¥2,500, based on the five-year average P/E of about 10x and the average of our EPS estimates for FY12/26 and FY12/27 of ¥253. Over the longer term, we anticipate synergies from active tread/Synchro Weather technology and the Dunlop brand, but see downside risks in tariffs and other political developments, the rising costs of raw materials including higher prices for natural rubber and crude oil, rising freight rates, and stiffer competition across the industry.

# AGC Inc (5201 / ASGLF)

Our ¥7,500 PO is derived from sum-of-the-parts (SOTP) valuation using EV/EBITDA multiples for each business segment and our FY12/27 estimates. We use multiples based on segment peer averages (architectural glass 6x, automotive 6x, electronics 15x, chemicals 5.5x, and life science 6x. Our PO equates to a P/E of 16.9x on our FY12/27 EPS estimate). We use SOTP for our valuation because of the variation by business segment in growth potential, profitability and risks. We also apply a 20% conglomerate discount based on the unlikelihood of synergies within AGC's business portfolio. Upside risks to our PO are (1) unexpectedly high growth in demand for EUV mask blanks and

biopharma CDMO services, (2) better headway in raising display glass prices than we expect, (3) spikes in PVC and caustic soda prices, and (4) sharp yen depreciation. Downside risks are (1) lower demand than expected for EUV mask blanks and biopharma CDMO services, (2) a sharp downturn in display glass prices, (3) a steep decline in PVC and caustic soda prices, and (4) sharp yen appreciation

# Nippon Electric Glass (5214 / NPEGF)

Our price objective of ¥8,000 is based on a P/E of 18x our FY12/27 EPS estimate. A P/E of 18x adds a 30% premium to the past five-year average. We consider an increase in valuations for AI-related stocks.

Upside risks for our PO are (1) avoiding a decline in demand for end-products (e.g., televisions, semiconductors, and automobiles) due to the repeal of US-China tariffs or other factors (2) greater-than-expected growth in the advanced semiconductor market (3) additional price hikes for displays and (4) improvement in the competitive environment for composite materials. Downside risks are (1) a greater-than-expected decline in end-product demand due to US-China tariffs (indirect impact from volume declines for televisions, semiconductors, automobiles, and other products) (2) weakness in the advanced semiconductor market (3) price cuts for displays and (4) worsening competitive environment for composite materials.

# Sumitomo Osaka Cement (5232 / SUCEF)

We calculate our ¥3,500 PO from our FY3/27 EPS forecast and a P/E of 9.5x, which is a roughly 10% discount to the company's five-year average P/E. We take into account the deteriorating outlook for domestic cement, the company's largest business. Potential upside catalysts for the share price are (1) a recovery in domestic cement demand, (2) announcement of additional cement price hikes, (3) substantial enhancements to shareholder returns, (4) a sharp decline in coal prices, (5) progress with industry consolidation, and (6) rapid yen appreciation. Downside risks are (1) even further weakness in domestic cement demand, (2) failure to raise cement prices, (3) deterioration in shareholder returns, (4) sharp rise in coal prices, (5) stalled industry reorganization, and (6) sharp yen depreciation.

# MARUWA (5344 / MAWAF)

Our ¥93,000 price objective is based on a 33x P/E and our FY3/28 EPS forecast. We referenced the share-price levels seen from 3Q FY3/26 onward, when yield concerns surrounding 1.6T AI-server heat-dissipation substrate production had eased to some extent and the market had begun to factor in earnings expansion from FY3/27 onward.

Downside risks are (1) slowing generative AI demand, (2) the emergence of rivals that erode Maruwa's high value-added heat dissipation substrates' competitiveness, (3) prolonged weakness in the Chinese economy, (4) receding automobile electrification, (5) difficulties in breaking into new markets to follow AI, and (6) rapid yen appreciation (OP sensitivity to the USD is tens of millions of yen).

# Nichias (5393 / NICFF)

Our price objective of ¥3,500 is based on a P/E of 20.0x our FY3/27 EPS estimate. This represents a level above the past 10-year P/E range of 7-13x, reflecting (1) prospects for the operating margin to increase, driven mainly by a return to a growth phase for SPE-related products, and (2) expectations for expanding shareholder returns. Upside risks for our PO are (1) a sharp rise in semiconductor demand, (2) acceleration of nuclear power restarts, and (3) expansion of shareholder returns. Downside risks are (1) a sharp decline in semiconductor demand, (2) stalled progress in nuclear restarts, and (3) contraction of shareholder returns.

# Shizuoka Financial Group (5831 / SFGIF)

Our PO of ¥3,100 for Shizuoka FG is based on applying a fair P/B of 1.26x (FY3/27-FY3/28 average estimate ROE of 8.17%, theoretical cost of capital of 6.47%, ROE estimates weighted at 75% for FY3/27 and 25% for FY3/28 to our FY3/27 BPS estimate of ¥2,471. The 6.47% cost of capital we use is based on an implied risk premium of 7.39%, calculated from the averages since the BoJ's adoption of its negative interest rate policy in 2016 of the risk premium for Japanese stocks of 4.82%, of the risk-free rate of 2.25%, and of the weekly beta of 1.067. To this we apply a 12.5% premium to reflect qualitative factors (including the long-term attractiveness of the business portfolio, capital policies, governance, and regulatory risks) and the high percentage of its stock owned by life insurers (and thus high selling pressure).

Upside risks to our price objective are (1) a stronger-than-expected economic recovery (lower-than-expected credit costs) in Shizuoka Prefecture, (2) faster-than-expected growth in shareholder payouts, and (3) better-than-expected results from alliances and growth investments. Downside risks: (4) Weaker-than-expected Shizuoka Prefecture economy (loan stagnation/higher credit costs), (5) slower-than-expected increase in shareholder payouts, and (6) failure to restructure the market division's securities portfolio (resulting in significant bond losses).

# NHK Spring (5991 / NHKGF)

Our 12-month PO for NHK Spring is ¥3,400. For our valuation P/E, we start with the roughly 17x FY/27 consensus P/E for leading peer, to which we apply a 15% discount to reflect the uncertain outlook for the autos business and the risk of a deterioration in supply/demand caused by a reduction in cross-shareholdings. We apply the resulting multiple to our EPS estimate for FY3/27, when we expect its main customer's production reductions to have run their course. Downside risks are changes in the external environment including forex and the macro economy, escalation in competition, and company-specific factors: recalls.

# Recruit Holdings (6098 / RCRRF)

Our PO of ¥12,000 is based on applying a 27x PER to our FY3/27 EPS estimate of ¥453, when we expect a step-up in earnings growth. The applied multiple is near the upper end of the 15-33x P/E range observed during 2020-22 when growth expectations were elevated.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) a delay in recovery in the HR Technology segment, (2) slower improvement in Market Matching Technologies profitability due to intensifying competition, and (3) changes in cash management.

# Hitachi (6501 / HTHIF / HTHIY)

The price objective of ¥6,000 (ADR: USD 38.7) is derived using an SOTP methodology based on FY3/27E EV/EBITDA. Digital Systems & Services (DSS) is assigned 13x, a 2pt premium to solution-based peers, reflecting growth opportunities in digital infrastructure such as Energy. Energy is valued at 21x, in line with peers. Mobility is assigned 10x (c.5pt premium) reflecting its digital strategy. Connective Industries (CI) is valued at a 2-3pt discount to peers, reflecting an ongoing portfolio strategy review.

Downside risks to our PO are: 1) faster-than-expected substitution of legacy IT services by AI, 2) a greater-than-expected deterioration in profitability owing to rising component costs, 3) weaker earnings caused by macroeconomic changes or geopolitical risk, and 4) unexpected losses on projects.

Upside risks are: 1) further acceleration in Lumada revenue growth, 2) Energy revenue and profitability improving faster than expected, 3) CI profitability improving faster than our assumptions owing to business portfolio reforms, and 4) further expansion of shareholder returns supported by a strengthened balance sheet and cash flow.

# Mitsubishi Electric (6503 / MIELF)

We calculate our ¥6,700 price objective from a sum-of-the-parts (SOTP) method based on our FY3/27 EV/EBITDA forecasts.

We apply 22x to Infrastructure, a c.10% discount to peers to reflect scale differences in defence and power. FA is valued at 22x, in line with the average of three domestic peers. Automotive Equipment uses 6x, in line with domestic peers and reflecting improved profitability, Life uses 15x, in line with the average of global peers, Digital Innovation uses 9x, a 1.5-4.5-point discount to several domestic peers reflecting business scale, and Semiconductors/Devices are valued at 18x, a 2-point premium to peers reflecting the growth potential of optical devices.

Upside risks to our PO are (1) earnings expansion driven by structural reform, (2) growth in the defence business, (3) a sharp recovery in the FA market, and (4) improved shareholder returns. Downside risks are (1) a slowdown in cyclical businesses, including FA, due to a global economic slowdown, (2) a greater-than-expected rise in raw material costs, (3) delays in business structural reform, and (4) a wider impact from the Iran war.

# Makita (6586 / MKEWF)

We base our ¥6,500 PO on an EV/EBIT of 13x on the average of our FY3/26-27 earnings estimates. Our 13x multiple is 1pt below the mid-cycle multiple based on the past two earnings cycle. We apply a modest discount to reflect the fact that Makita's returns (e.g. RoE's) have not quite returned to previous trends yet.

Downside risks are worse-than-expected weakening in end-market demand and return of excess promotional spending.

# NEC (6701 / NIPNF)

The price objective of ¥6,900 is based on our FY3/28E EPS of ¥263.9 and a P/E of 26x. This multiple is above the midpoint of the historical five-year forward P/E range of 11-30x. We view this valuation as justified, reflecting the visibility of structural reform benefits, progress in improving IT services profitability, and expansion in defense-related businesses.

Upside risks to our price objective are as follows: (1) stronger-than-expected margin expansion and increased domestic IT services orders, (2) clearer quantitative upside from AI utilization, (3) expansion of the defense budget, (4) M&A with clear synergies, and (5) an earlier recovery and growth in the subsea cable business.

Downside risks to our price objective are: (1) a slowdown in margin improvement and (2) weak domestic IT services order trends.

# Fujitsu (6702 / FJTSF / FJTSY)

Price objective of ¥4,800 (ADR: \$30.97) is based on our FY3/28E EPS of ¥200.9 and a P/E of 24x. The 24x multiple reflects an approximately 10% discount to peers (26x), taking into account relative growth prospects. We view this valuation as justified given strong earnings growth and solid shareholder returns. While 24x is close to the upper end of the past five-year forward P/E range (12-25x), the historically low ceiling was driven by the inclusion of non-core earnings and gains from divestitures of subsidiaries and affiliates.

Upside risks to our price objective are: (1) A next medium-term plan (scheduled for May 28, 2026) that exceeds market expectations, (2) Clear evidence of quantifiable upside from AI deployment, (3) Sustained strong revenue growth in service solutions, and (4) Limited compensation costs related to the UK Post Office issue

Downside risks are: (1) Weak domestic order intake, (2) Company guidance with significant downside risk, and (3) Compensation costs related to the UK Post Office issue materially exceeding market expectations

# Renesas Electronics (6723 / RNECF)

Our PO for Renesas is ¥4,900, calculated by applying EV/EBITDA of 16.5x our FY12/26 estimates (non-GAAP). 16.5x multiple is par with Renesas' global peer.

Upside risks to our price objective are 1) greater-than-expected improvement in prices and the cost structure, 2) sharp growth in the market for automotive and industrial semiconductors, 3) greater-than-expected top-line synergies with acquired companies, and 4) a rise in the share of the growing segments of the automotive market (ADAS/EV). Downside risks are 1) a deterioration in semiconductor market conditions owing to worse-than-expected reductions of global auto production and macroeconomic weakness, 2) asset-impairment losses owing to weaker-than-expected earnings in businesses that the company has aggressively acquired, and 3) the negative earnings impact of yen appreciation.

# Panasonic (6752 / PCRFF)

The price objective of ¥3,800 is derived using an SOTP methodology based on our FY3/27E EV/EBITDA. We apply 9x to Connect (c.4pt discount to Mitsubishi Electric and Thales) reflecting uncertainty around Blue Yonder. Electric Works is valued at 7x (1pt premium to Signify) on growth in wiring devices. HVAC&CC (Heating, Ventilation, Air Conditioning & Cold Chain) is assigned 6x (c.3pt discount to Daikin) considering differences in scale and profitability. Energy is valued at 29x (c.4pt discount to Taiwan's Delta Electronics), reflecting uncertainty in EV battery demand. Industry is assigned 13x, in line with the lower end of the peer range, factoring in relatively weaker competitiveness in FA solutions. Smart Life is valued at 5.5x, in line with the average of global appliance peers (Whirlpool, Electrolux, LG Electronics).

Downside risks to our price objective are (1) a global economic slowdown driven by geopolitical risks, (2) a prolonged consumption slump owing to domestic economic stagnation, and (3) lower profitability owing to tougher-than-assumed competition in the battery business. Upside risks are (1) stronger-than-assumed profitability improvement driven by structural reforms, (2) further narrowing of the business portfolio, and (3) a recovery in demand for the automotive battery business.

# DENSO (6902 / DNZOF)

Our 12-month PO for Denso is ¥2,400. For our valuation P/B, we start with the FY3/21-25 average of 1.2x. While major OEM-affiliated suppliers may enjoy a relative advantage in terms of volumes, uncertainty surrounding the potential tender offer for Rohm leads us to apply no valuation premium or discount. The price objective is derived by applying the target P/B multiple to FY2026/3 Q3 reported BPS. Downside risks include sharp production cuts driven by rising raw material costs and supply-chain disruptions, intensifying competition, and shifts in expectations for electrification. In addition, potential M&A in the semiconductor space could represent either an upside or downside risk.

# Kawasaki Heavy Industries (7012 / KWHIF)

Our PO of ¥3,760 derives from an EV/EBITDA multiple of 11x applied to our FY3/28 earnings forecasts. The shares are currently trading above the 10-year average multiple of approx. 10x and we assign a premium based on our view that the company's earnings cycle is bottoming, creating room for the stock to re-rerate.

Downside risks to our PO are (1) an unexpectedly sharp slowdown in motorcycle/RV sales in the US, (2) a lack of cyclical recovery in robotics and hydraulics, and (3) heavier US tariffs than expected. Greater strength than expected in cyclical businesses (Powersports & Engine, Precision Machinery & Robot) is an upside risk.

# IHI (7013 / IHICF)

Our PO of ¥4,950 derives from an EV/EBITDA multiple of 19x applied to the average of our FY3/28 earnings forecasts. The multiple is a 1ppt discount to the average of its

global peers in aerospace which are trading on average EV/EBITDA multiple of around 20x. We believe the discount is appropriate given that IHI is not a "pure play aerospace company."

Downside risks to our PO are (1) the extension of the US slowdown in domestic air travel to other markets, and (2) a rapid reversal of the spare parts business profit inflation in FY3/25 arising from delayed cost recognition. Upside risks are (1) improvement in airline passenger traffic, and (2) continued restructuring in the Industrial Systems and General-Purpose Machinery segment, in particular.

# Mebuki Financial Group (7167 / MEBUF)

Our ¥1,650 price objective is derived by applying a theoretical P/B of 1.33x to FY2027E book value per share of ¥1,228, based on an average FY2027-FY2028E ROE of 10.11% and a theoretical cost of equity of 7.63%. We weight ROE assumptions 75% for FY2027 and 25% for FY2028.

The 7.63% cost of equity reflects an implied risk premium of 7.44%, calculated from the post-2016 average Japan equity risk premium of 4.82%, average risk-free rate of 2.25%, and average weekly beta of 1.077, with a 2.5% qualitative discount applied for factors including long-term business portfolio attractiveness, capital policy, governance, and regulatory risk.

Upside risks to the price objective are: (1) stronger-than-expected economic recovery in Ibaraki and Tochigi, (2) faster-than-expected expansion of shareholder returns, and (3) better-than-expected securities-related gains. Downside risks are: (1)

weaker-than-expected regional economic conditions, (2) slower-than-expected shareholder return expansion, and (3) weaker-than-expected securities-related gains.

# Yokohama Financial Group (7186 / CCRDF)

We set our price objective for Yokohama Financial Group at ¥1,700, based on our FY3/27 BPS estimate of ¥1,296 and theoretical P/B of 1.32x (FY3/27-FY3/28 average estimate ROE 9.11% / theoretical cost of capital 6.91%, ROE estimates weighted at 75% for FY3/27 and 25% for FY3/28). Our theoretical cost of capital at 6.91% is based on an implied risk premium of 8.23%, derived from an average risk premium of 4.82% for Japanese stocks since NIRP adoption in 2016, risk free rate of 2.25%, and average weekly beta of 1.241, to which we apply a 16.0% premium in view of qualitative factors (including long-term attractiveness of business portfolio, capital policy, governance, and regulatory risk).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) a stronger-than-expected economic recovery in Kanagawa Prefecture (e.g., lending growth and lower credit costs), (2) faster-than-expected growth in shareholder returns, and (3) higher-than-expected growth in corporate fee income (a strength derived from the bank's geographically deep customer marketing base).

Downside risks are (1) weaker-than-expected economic conditions in Kanagawa Prefecture (e.g., weak lending and higher credit costs), (2) slower-than-expected growth in shareholder returns, (4) failed securities portfolio restructuring in the markets division (large bond losses), and failed M&A.

# Nissan Motor (7201 / NSANF / NSANY)

We set our price objective for Nissan shares at ¥390 (ADR: \$4.8). Our PO basis P/B multiple is based on the FY3/21-FY3/25 average of 0.4x, adjusted to reflect risks that could erode book value per share (BPS). These are: concerns over volume deterioration following tariff-related cost burdens and price increases, potential for special losses associated with structural reforms, and broader concerns about financial resilience, as seen in rising CDS spreads. We calculate the price objective by applying the PO basis P/B to a BPS that incorporates a risk of approximately 30% erosion in shareholders' equity. Upside risks are scenarios where Nissan becomes the subject of another tender offer, a different company steps in, or changes occur in partnership structures, any of which could drive share price movements that diverge from fundamentals. Additionally, if currency trends, macroeconomic conditions, and debates around tariffs and

electrification stabilize, or if the company executes significant structural reforms, expectations for a recovery in fundamentals could lift the share price. Downside risks are currency volatility, macroeconomic instability, unfavorable developments in tariff or electrification discussions, or a deterioration in expectations for fundamental recovery.

# Toyota Motor (7203 / TOYOF / TM)

Our price objective for Toyota shares is ¥4,200 (ADR: \$269.10). The PO basis P/B multiple reflects a 40% valuation premium to the FY3/21-FY3/25 average of 1.0x, taking into account our view that profitability can be maintained even with tariff burdens, as well as potential ROE improvement from share buybacks and cancellations, and the company's strength in HEVs. The price objective is calculated by applying this multiple to 3Q FY3/26 BPS. Downside risks are sharp currency fluctuations, changes in raw material prices, government policies in various countries, economic trends, and other macro business environment factors.

# Mitsubishi Motors (7211 / MMTOF)

Our 12-month PO for Mitsubishi Motors is ¥360. For our valuation P/B, we start with the FY3/21-25 average multiple of 0.8x, then reflect a 30% discount given that the company does not have a US plant and may face challenges in responding to tariffs, as well as concerns about a slower-than-expected recovery in ASEAN sales and intensifying competition. We apply this multiple to 3Q FY3/26 BPS. Upside risks are progress on integration with Honda and Nissan, heightened speculation regarding accelerated restructuring, and forex swings.

# Mazda Motor (7261 / MZDAF)

Our 12-month PO for Mazda is ¥1,310. The target P/B is based on the 21/3-25/3 average of 0.5x. While we have previously applied a discount to reflect risks of shareholder equity erosion from tariffs and other factors, the company has achieved profitability, visibility on margin improvement has improved, and overall market valuations have risen. Based on this, we apply a justified PBR with a zero premium or discount and derive the target price by applying it to BPS. Upside risks are policy changes such as lower tariffs, a rise in Toyota's equity stake, and heightened speculation around accelerated restructuring. Downside risks are a capital increase targeted at US capacity expansion, forex swings, and materials price fluctuations.

# Honda Motor (7267 / HNDAF / HMC)

Our 12-month price objective for Honda is ¥1,600 (ADR: \$29.83). The target P/B is based on the 21/3-25/3 average of 0.5x. While profitability has been maintained even after factoring in tariff burdens and there is scope for a re-rating of the motorcycle and financial services businesses, we apply a 5% valuation discount to reflect intensifying competition in the core US market and uncertainty surrounding the electrification strategy, and derive the target price by applying this multiple to actual 3Q FY2026/3 BPS. Downside risks are macroeconomic fluctuations and policy changes such as tariffs, as well as developments in industry restructuring, sharp currency fluctuations, and volatility in raw material prices.

# Suzuki Motor (7269 / SZKMF)

Our 12-month PO for Suzuki is ¥2,500. For our valuation P/B, we start with the FY3/21-25 average of 1.15x, to which we apply a 25% premium to reflect 1) the limited direct impact from US tariffs and 2) the benefit to the India market from GST reductions. We apply the resulting multiple to 3Q FY3/26 BPS. Downside risks are India stock market movements, forex swings, and materials price fluctuations.

# Subaru Corp (7270 / FUJHF / FUJHY)

Our price objective for Subaru is ¥3,200 (ADR: \$10.06). We set the PO basis P/B at the 21/3-25/3 average of 0.8x with a zero valuation premium or discount, and the price objective is derived by applying this multiple to actual 3Q FY2026/3 BPS. The upside risk is the improvement in the earnings outlook owing to the significant easing of US tariffs. Downside risks are sudden exchange rate fluctuations and fluctuations in raw material procurement prices.

# Koito Manufacturing (7276 / KOTMF)

Our 12-month PO for Koito is ¥3,100. For our valuation P/B, we start with the FY3/21-25 average of 1.3x, to which we had applied a 5% discount to reflect the relatively low weighting of sales to Toyota compared to other Toyota suppliers and the slow recovery in margins post-COVID. However, as we are now seeing signs of margin improvement, including a reduction in LiDAR losses, we assume this discount at zero. We apply the resulting multiple to 4Q FY3/26 BPS. Downside risks are changes in government policies on tariffs and safety in various countries, changes in the external environment, in particular forex and the macro economy, deterioration in LiDAR and other new businesses, escalation in competition, and company specific factors such as recalls.

# Daishi Hokuetsu Financial Group (7327 / DSHHF)

Our price objective for Daishi Hokuetsu FG of ¥2,400 is based on our end-FY3/27 BPS estimate of ¥2,219 and a P/B of 1.08x (derived from a cost of capital of 8.30% and FY3/26-3/28 average ROE of 8.99% based on our estimates, weighted at 75% for FY3/27 and 25% for FY3/28. The 8.30% cost of capital we use is based on an implied risk premium of 7.90%, calculated from the averages since the BoJ adopted negative interest rate policy (NIRP) in 2016 of a Japanese equity risk premium of 4.82%, of the risk-free rate of 2.25%, and of the weekly beta of 1.173, to which we apply an 5.0% discount to reflect qualitative factors (including the long-term attractiveness of the business portfolio, capital policies, governance, and regulatory risks).

Upside risks to the price objective are: (1) A stronger-than-expected recovery in the Niigata Prefecture economy, (2) a faster-than-expected pace of shareholder return expansion, and (3) greater-than-expected synergies from the M&A with Daishi Hokuetsu FG. Downside risks are: (1) A weaker-than-expected Niigata Prefecture economy, (2) a slower-than-expected pace of shareholder return expansion, and (3)

higher-than-expected M&A costs and lower-than-expected synergies or cancellation of the planned integration with Gunma Bank.

# Ryohin Keikaku (7453 / RYKKF / RYKKY)

We set our PO at ¥4,500 (ADR: US\$14.52), based on c.32x our FY8/28 estimate P/E. In setting our PO, we refer to the valuation seen in 1H18, when the stock traded at a premium of slightly more than 30% to TOPIX Retail, reflecting the market's positive view of both domestic and overseas growth, supported not only by Japan but also by strong performance in China.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) the possibility that the supermarket-adjacent format could become a new growth driver (2) a scenario in which cost improvements exceed our expectations as the supply chain is strengthened and (3) a scenario in which the equity market renews its expectations for overseas growth. Downside risks, on the other hand, are: (1) a scenario in which overseas operations, including China, underperform our expectations (2) a scenario in which sales struggle due to external factors such as weather and (3) a scenario in which management's time horizon differs from that expected by the equity market.

# Pan Pacific International HD (7532 / DQJCF)

We set our PO at ¥1,150, based on c.26x our FY6/28E P/E. In setting our PO, we take reference from the fact that the stock traded broadly in line with TOPIX Retail during 2024-2025, when the market increasingly recognized the company's strengthening competitive edge as a discounter.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) an acceleration in the company's sales growth driven by the implementation of a tax cut on food and beverages, (2) an earlier-than-expected recovery in inbound visitors from China, and (3) a higher contribution from, and/or

increasing expectations for, the company's new store formats.

Downside risks are: (1) potential near-term earnings pressure from upfront investments such as store openings and M&A, (2) a scenario where talent development fails to keep pace with the company's growth trajectory, and (3) a sharper-than-expected intensification of price competition in the domestic market.

# Shimadzu (7701 / SHMZF)

Price objective is ¥5,300, which we base on an EV/EBITDA of 13.0x and our FY3/27 forecasts. 13.0x is slightly higher than 12.7x which is the upper end of the last 2-year range of two-year forward EV/EBITDA.

Upside risks are (1) earlier-than-expected recovery in demand for China business, and (2) greater-than-expected contribution from new client base/new business in North America and in the healthcare field.

Downside risks are (1) decrease in analytical equipment demand because of economic fluctuations, (2) greater-than-expected increase in R&D costs, (3) increase in investments including human capital and marketing costs, and (4) geopolitical risks from high exposure to China.

# Nakanishi (7716 / NKNSF)

We derive our ¥3,900 PO by applying FY12/27 EV/EBITDA of 10.5x. We view 10.5x to be achievable as conviction regarding a return to growth strengthens, given it is broadly in line with the median of the past three-year trading range. Downside risks to our PO are (1) pricing competition originating in the low-end segment of dental spreads into the mid- to high-end segment faster than expected, (2) margins on DSO/OEM products decline more than we assume, and (3) surgical market penetration falls short of expectations due to a failure in the partner strategy.

# Menicon (7780 / MNNLF)

The ¥2,200 PO is derived by applying a FY3/28 EV/EBITDA multiple of 8.1x. The 8.1x multiple represents a 5% discount to the 8.5x upper end of the past two-year range. We believe 8.5x should ultimately be achievable as sales growth and margin improvement from 1Day expansion become clearer and confidence in growth increases, but we apply a 5% discount to reflect delays versus medium-term plan. Downside risks to our PO are: (1) delays in the ramp-up of the Malaysia plant, (2) margin improvement from a higher 1Day sales mix falling short of our assumptions, and (3) overcapacity emerging earlier than expected due to accelerated capex by competitors.

# Bandai Namco Holdings (7832 / NCBDF)

Our PO of ¥3,610 is derived by applying a multiple of 18.4x to our FY3/27 EPS forecast of ¥196. This represents a c.20% discount to the FY26 average PER of 23x for 13 game and content companies under our coverage. We apply this discount as we forecast the company's EPS CAGR for FY3/28-FY3/29 at c.2%, below the peer average of c.12%.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) the creation of blockbuster titles in the game business and (2) the launch of new hit products in the toy business. Downside risks are: (1) opportunity loss due to intensifying competition for content acquisition, (2) a sharp deterioration in mobile game sales, and (3) inventory write-downs in the toy business.

# Asics (7936 / ASCCF)

We set our PO at ¥6,000, based on c.33x FY12/27 P/E. In setting our PO, we reference the valuation environment in 2025, when the company's 12-month forward PER traded at a c.10-20% premium to TOPIX Retail, supported by sales growth in performance running as well as sports style and Onitsuka Tiger.

Upside risks are the establishment of new product categories, contributions from digital initiatives and the running ecosystem, and a scenario in which concerns over tariffs ease further. Downside risks are intensifying competition with peers, a slowdown in sales due to changes in consumer trends, and a scenario in which yen appreciation reduces inbound demand from overseas.

# Pigeon (7956 / PIGEF)

We derive a PO of ¥2,000, based on P/B-ROE correlation, by applying a P/B multiple of 3.6x (based on our FY26/12E ROE of 14.2%) to our FY26/12E BPS. Downside risks are a deterioration in profitability in the China market, supply constraints due to procurement difficulties for petroleum derived materials, and further intensification of competition in key operating regions.

# Nintendo (7974 / NTDOF)

Our price objective of ¥10,700 is derived from a 10-year DCF model covering FY3/26-FY3/36. Our assumptions include a risk-free rate of 2.0%, based on the 10-year Japanese government bond yield, an equity risk premium of 6.0%, a terminal growth rate of 0%, an equity beta of 0.8, and a WACC of 6.6%. Our price objective implies approximately 17x our forecast peak EPS of ¥631 in FY3/29.

Risks to our price objective are weaker-than-expected sales of Switch 2, delays in the release of anticipated game titles, intensifying competition from peers, rising memory prices, and tariffs.

# Credit Saison (8253 / CSASF)

Our PO of ¥5,300 is based on a P/B of 0.95x (assuming adjusted ROE of around 9.5%, and a 10% cost of capital) and our end-FY3/27 BPS estimate.

Downside risks for our price objective are (1) a sharp increase in defaults in India and Vietnam, (2) a rapid decline in merchant fees in Japan, (3) a sudden slowdown in the growth of domestic revolving and installment receivables, mortgages, and annual card fee growth.

Upside risks are (1) a significant increase in the dividend payout ratio, currently 27%, (2) stronger-than-expected growth in India, Brazil, and other countries, and (3) stronger-than-expected growth in domestic revolving and installment receivables and mortgages.

# Mitsubishi UFJ Financial Group (8306 / MBFJF / MUFG)

Our PO for MUFG of ¥3,450 (ADR \$22.25) is based on applying a fair P/B of 1.80x (FY3/27-FY3/28 average estimate ROE of 12.42% and theoretical cost of capital of 6.92%, ROE estimates weighted at 75% in FY3/27 and 25% in FY3/28) to our FY3/27 BPS estimate of ¥1,926. The 6.92% cost of capital we use is based on an implied risk premium of 8.38%, calculated from the averages (since the BoJ's adoption of NIRP in 2016) of the risk premium for Japan stocks of 4.82%, of the risk-free rate of 2.25%, and of the weekly beta of 1.272, to which we apply a 17.5% premium to reflect qualitative factors (including the long-term attractiveness of the business portfolio, capital policies, governance and regulatory risks).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) faster-than-expected business portfolio reshuffling and ROE improvement, (2) faster-than-expected growth in shareholder returns, and (3) more-than-expected yen depreciation and improvement in US MS earnings. Downside risks are (1) greater-than-expected earnings deterioration at US MS and partner banks in Southeast Asia, (2) slower-than-expected growth in shareholder returns and missteps with M&A, and (3) more yen appreciation than expected.

# Resona Holdings (8308 / RSNHF)

Our PO for Resona of ¥2,350 is based on applying a fair P/B of 1.74x (FY3/27-FY3/28 average estimate ROE of 11.41% and theoretical cost of capital of 6.54%, ROE estimates weighted at 75% for FY3/27 and 25% for FY3/28) to our FY3/27 BPS estimate of ¥1,362. The 6.54% cost of capital we use is based on an implied risk premium of 7.93%, calculated from the averages (since the BoJ's adoption of NIRP in 2016) of the risk premium for Japan stocks of 4.82%, of the risk-free rate of 2.25%, and of the weekly beta of 1.178, to which we apply a 17.5% premium to reflect qualitative factors (including the long-term attractiveness of the business portfolio, capital policies, governance, and regulatory risks).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) greater-than-expected fee income growth, (2) a greater-than-expected increase in net interest income owing to BoJ policy adjustments, and (3) faster-than-expected growth in shareholder payouts. Downside risks are (1) weaker-than-expected momentum in fee income, (2) weaker-than-expected momentum in net interest income, and (3) weaker-than-expected growth in shareholder payouts.

# Sumitomo Mitsui Financial Group (8316 / SMFNF / SMFG)

Our PO for SMFG of ¥6,650 (ADR \$25.84) is based on applying a fair P/B of 1.57x (FY3/27-FY3/28 average estimate ROE of 11.31% and theoretical cost of capital of 7.22%, ROE estimates weighted at 75% for FY3/27 and 25% for FY3/28) to our FY3/27 BPS estimate of ¥4.254. The 7.22% cost of capital we use is based on an implied risk premium of 8.02%, calculated from the averages (since the BoJ's adoption of NIRP in 2016) of the risk premium for Japan stocks of 4.82%, of the risk-free rate of 2.25%, and of the weekly beta of 1.197, to which we apply a 10.0% premium to reflect qualitative factors (including the long-term attractiveness of the business portfolio, capital policies, governance and regulatory risks).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) a faster-than-expected recovery of its mainstay consumer finance, credit card, and asset financing businesses, (2) faster-than-expected increases in shareholder payouts, and (3) faster earnings improvement at SMBC Nikko Securities and the US capital markets business. Downside risks are (1) a slower-than-expected recovery of its consumer finance, credit card, and asset financing businesses, (2) slower-than-expected increases in shareholder payouts and overpayment for acquisitions, and (3) weak earnings improvement at SMBC Nikko Securities and relative weakness in its US capital markets business.

# Chiba Bank (8331 / CHBAF)

Our price objective for Chiba of ¥2,500 is based on applying a fair P/B of 1.32x (FY3/27-FY3/28 average estimate ROE of 8.74% and theoretical cost of capital of 6.64%, ROE estimates weighted at 75% for FY3/27 and 25% for FY3/28) to our FY3/27 BPS estimate of ¥1,902. The 6.64% cost of capital we use is based on an implied risk premium of 8.19%, calculated from the averages since the BoJ's adoption of NIRP in 2016 of the risk premium for Japan stocks of 4.82%, of the risk-free rate of 2.25%, and of the weekly beta of 1.233, to which we apply a 19.0% premium to reflect qualitative factors (including the long-term attractiveness of the business portfolio, capital policies, governance, regulatory risks, and the large stakes owned by life insurers (the high pressure they are under to sell the holdings)).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) a stronger-than-expected recovery in Chiba prefecture's economy (implications for lending growth, credit cost downside), (2) greater-than-expected increases in shareholder returns, and (3) higher-than-expected revenue from the bank's cashless and alliance strategies, and strong retail sales. Downside risks are (1) a weaker-than-expected economy in Chiba prefecture (sluggish lending, higher-than-expected credit costs), (2) slower-than-expected increases in shareholder returns, and (3) mistakes in rejigging the securities portfolio in the markets division (incurrence of heavy bond losses).

# Gunma Bank (8334 / GMBKF)

Our PO for Gunma Bank of ¥2,700 is based on our end-FY3/27 BPS estimate of ¥1,674 and a P/B of 1.57x (derived from a cost of capital of 6.99% and FY3/27-3/28 average ROE of 10.97% based on our estimates, weighted at 75% for FY3/27 and 25% for FY3/28. The 6.99% cost of capital we use is based on an implied risk premium of 7.76%, calculated from the averages since the BoJ adopted negative interest-rate policy (NIRP) in 2016 of a Japanese equity risk premium of 4.83%, of the risk-free rate of 2.25%, and of the weekly beta of 1.143, to which we apply an 10.0% premium to reflect qualitative factors (including the long-term attractiveness of the business portfolio, capital policies, governance, and regulatory risks).

Upside risks to the price objective are: (1) A stronger-than-expected recovery in the Gunma Prefecture economy, (2) a faster-than-expected pace of shareholder return expansion, and (3) greater-than-expected synergies from the M&A with Daishi Hokuetsu

FG. Downside risks are: (1) A weaker-than-expected Gunma Prefecture economy, (2) a slower-than-expected pace of shareholder return expansion, and (3) higher-than-expected M&A costs and lower-than-expected synergies or cancellation of the planned integration with Daishi Hokuetsu FG.

# Fukuoka Financial Group (8354 / FKKFF)

We set our price objective for Fukuoka Financial Group at ¥6,850, based on our FY3/27 BPS estimate of ¥5,876 and theoretical P/B of 1.16x (FY3/27-FY3/28 average estimate ROE 8.32% / theoretical cost of capital 9.67%, ROE estimates weighted at 75% for FY3/27 and 25% for FY3/28). Our theoretical cost of capital at 9.67% is based on an implied risk premium of 8.32%, derived from an average risk premium of 4.82% for Japanese stocks since NIRP adoption in 2016, risk free rate of 2.25%, and average weekly beta of 1.286, to which we apply a 1.5% premium in view of qualitative factors (including long-term attractiveness of business portfolio, capital policy, governance, and regulatory risk).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) a stronger-than-expected economic recovery in Kyushu (e.g., lending growth and lower credit costs), (2) faster-than-expected growth in shareholder returns, and (3) greater-than-expected earnings improvement at Minna Bank and lower losses due to business exits and other actions. Downside risks are (4) weaker-than-expected economic conditions in Kyushu (e.g., weak lending and higher credit costs), (5) slower-than-expected growth in shareholder returns, (6) greater-than-expected earnings deterioration at Minna Bank and slow decisions on business exits and other actions, and (7) failed securities portfolio restructuring in the markets division (large bond losses).

# Mizuho Financial Group (8411 / MZHOF / MFG)

Our PO for Mizuho Financial Group of ¥8,300 (ADR \$10.81) is based on applying a fair P/B of 1.75x (FY3/27-FY3/28 average estimate ROE of 12.19% and theoretical cost of capital of 6.98%, ROE estimates weighted at 75% for FY3/27 and 25% for FY3/28) to our FY3/27 BPS estimate of ¥4,750. The 6.98% cost of capital we use is based on an implied risk premium of 7.55%, calculated from the averages (since the BoJ's adoption of NIRP in 2016) of the risk premium for Japan stocks of 4.82%, of the risk-free rate of 2.25%, and of the weekly beta of 1.099, to which we apply a 7.5% premium to reflect qualitative factors (including the long-term attractiveness of the business portfolio, capital policies, governance and regulatory risks).

Upside risks to our PO are (1) share gains in the capital markets business on a pickup in improvement in business with large companies, (2) faster-than-expected growth in shareholder payouts, and (3) a faster-than-expected pace of business portfolio changes. Downside risks are (1) a worse-than-expected slowdown in the capital markets business, where the bank has strength, (2) weaker-than-expected growth in shareholder payouts and M&A failures, and (3) deterioration in the credit risk of large corporate clients and a failure to control unrealized losses on foreign bonds.

# ORIX (8591 / ORXCF / IX)

We assume ROE of at most 11% excluding contributions of a major Japanese conglomerate and a cost of equity of 10%, implying a P/B of 1.1x (¥5tn). The value of indirectly held flash memory specialist, based on consensus price objectives, is ¥900bn. For the conglomerate ex-the specialist, we assume 15% ownership and value it at 20x annualized H2 FY3/26 operating profit after tax, implying ¥770bn. Adding these components and converting to a per-share basis yields a price objective (PO) of ¥6,280 (ADR \$38.87).

Downside risks to our PO: (1) Far lower-than-expected capital gains from FY3/27, and (2) a sharp decline in the environment for US credit and private equity investment. Upside risks: (1) A rapid improvement in conditions in the US business, (2) the announcement of relatively large, attractive investments, and (3) a greater-than-expected increase in shareholder returns.

# Mitsubishi HC Capital (8593 / MIUFF)

Our price objective for Mitsubishi HC Capital is ¥1,310. We apply a P/B of 0.9x to our end-FY3/27 BPS forecast, based on our view that its ROE to remain at around 9% in the near term, while its cost of equity is close to 10%.

Upside risks to our PO: (1) a rapid improvement in credit costs and funding costs in Europe and North America, (2) a substantial recovery in profits at underperforming businesses such as environmental energy, and (3) a higher likelihood of significant profit improvement driven by the realization of the new medium-term plan, including a large reduction in low-ROA assets and an expansion of high-ROA assets. Downside risks: (1) a renewed deterioration in credit costs (European operations and North American commercial truck finance), (2) persistently elevated funding costs (UK and US operations), (3) a sharp decline in the currently high utilization rate owing to worsening performance in the logistics business, caused by a contraction in global trade volumes and North American logistics activity, and (4) increased earnings volatility owing to the timing of gains and losses on asset sales.

# Daiwa Group (8601 / DSECF)

Our PO of ¥1,670 is based on our BPS estimate for end-Mar 2027 and a P/B of 1.24x, derived from normalized ROE of 10.5% and a cost of capital of 8.5%.

Upside risks to our PO are (1) a significant change in shareholders' return policy to adjust it currently high CET1 ratio, (2) a rally on stock markets that results in strong growth in global market revenue and sales commissions from investment trusts, and (3) strong growth in customer numbers and earnings as a result of the alliance with a bank equity-method affiliate.

Downside risks are (1) deterioration in stock markets that leads to sharp declines in global market revenue and sales commissions from investment trusts, (2) investing further in a bank equity-method affiliate at a relatively high price to make it a fully owned subsidiary, as well as decisions to make other pricey investments, (3) significant deterioration in financial condition of investee entities under alternative investment business.

# Sompo Holdings (8630 / NHOLF)

We have a DDM-based price objective of ¥7,250. As management clarified an intention to raise dividend payout ratio from 3-year avg. adjusted profit to 50% by FY3/31, we assume DPS to grow ¥40-50 a year from FY3/27E of ¥200 until FY3/30 making payout ratio to 47%. Thereafter, we conservatively assume 3% growth. We use a discount rate of 4.25%, and applied 10% discount to the calculation result.

# Upside risks to our price objective.

(1) Dividend payout ratio to be raised faster than market expectation, (2) confirmation of a Japan profit recovery that exceeds company expectations, and (3) a clear indication that proceeds from the sale of strategic shareholdings will be allocated to worthwhile investment.

# Downside risks to our price objective.

(1) Japan profit recovery that falls behind plan, (2) loss of share amid coming changes in commercial property insurance marketing practices, (3) a deterioration in underwriting in the overseas business, which is currently strong, and 4) the announcement of M&A at high valuations.

# Japan Exchange Group (8697 / OSCUF)

We base our price objective of ¥2,200 on P/E of 23x and annualized EPS for 4Q FY3/26. Given the recent strong investor focus on Japanese equities, it is reasonable to use a P/E of 23x for our price objective, even if this sits toward the higher end of global exchange

valuations.

Upside risks for our price objective are 1) a sharp increase in trading value at Tokyo Stock Exchange, and 2) significant improvement in shareholder payouts.

Downside risks for our price objective are 1) a significant decline in trading value at Tokyo Stock Exchange, and 2) faster growth in IT expenses than revenue.

# Daiichi Life Group (8750 / DCNSF)

Our PO of ¥1,870 is based on DDM. We assume adjusted profit growth of 3% and apply 50% payout ratio based on the three-year average from FY3/30. We use a discount rate of 4.75%

Upside risks are (1) a larger-than-expected increase in new business in Japan, (2) greater-than-expected growth in overseas businesses, and (3) sharp rise in Japan equities and other risky assets.

Downside risks are (1) a sharp drop in new business, (2) the booking of significant losses on risk assets in Japan or overseas, (3) excessive flattening in the yen yield curve in the long to ultra-long zone, (4) a large-scale acquisition at a pricey valuation.

# Tokio Marine Holdings (8766 / TKOMF / TKOMY)

We base our price objective of ¥9,840 (ADR:\$61.96) on our DDM. We assume 9% growth of DPS from FY3/27 to FY3/29 factoring management explanation. Thereafter, we conservatively assume 3% DPS growth from FY3/30. For calculating an FY3/33 terminal value, we use a DPS of ¥417 and a discount rate of 4.0%.

Upside risks to our price objective are (1) management announcing more aggressive shareholders' return than what market expect, (2) growth of both domestic and foreign business profits exceeding market expectation, (3) announcement of a good M&A that will enable the company to grow overseas business profit faster.

Downside risks for our price objective: (1) Smaller improvement in shareholders return than what market expect, (2) Significant deterioration of US office CRE loan and private credit portfolio. (3) Yen appreciation of 10% would reduce adjusted profit (excluding gains on the sale of strategic shareholdings) by just over 7%. (4) Excessive softening of premium rates in the overseas business.

# Katitas (8919 / KTITF)

We calculate our ¥4,000 price objective using our FY3/28 EPS estimate and a P/E of 20x. The multiple is the company's average P/E based on the current year + 1-yr. forward Bloomberg consensus since January 2018 (immediately after the IPO).

Upside risks: (1) stronger demand for low-priced housing (properties with simple renovations), (2) an earlier-than-expected profit growth contribution from the introduction of mandatory real estate inheritance registration, and (3) M&A.

Downside risks: (1) ebbing in demand in the new-build/pre-owned property markets, (2) a slowdown in purchasing, (3) a sharp rise in short-term interest rates, and (4) up-spikes in materials and labor costs.

# Odakyu Railway (9007 / ODERF)

Our price objective of ¥2,000 is based on 1.4x P/B on our end-FY3/27 BPS estimate, which is broadly in line with historical average of Kanto region private rail peers.

Upside risks: 1) Future earnings growth beyond company guidance for FY3/26-27, 2) announcement of share buybacks to support higher-than-expected total shareholder return beyond company's 40% target, and 3) pricing reform announcement to boost railway profit.

Downside risks: 1) Labor shortage impacting rail and non-rail operation and increase of labor costs, 2) natural disasters impacting Odakyu's transportation operations, 3) sharp currency movement leading to inbound traffic decline, 4) higher material costs, and 5) liquidation of cross shareholdings.

# Nippon Yusen (9101 / NYUKF)

Our price objective of ¥6,525 is set at 0.9x March 2026 book. We set our target multiple at the upper end of NYK's trading range during the Red Sea closure since late 2023 to factor the near term spot rate inflection.

Upside risks: 1) stronger-than-expected global economic environment, boosting world trade volumes and liner rates, 2) better-than-expected capacity discipline by vessel operators/owners leading to an increase in freight rates, 3) higher synergies from ONE containership business, 4) a sharp decline in the cost of bunker fuel, and 5) a much weaker Japanese Yen given it is primarily a USD business.

Downside risks: 1) a deterioration in macro conditions causing world trade to weaken and rates to soften, 2) a further increase in trade tensions and tariffs, 3) lack of capacity discipline, prolonging and deepening the period of overcapacity in container and dry bulk, and 4) a stronger Japanese yen and/or high bunker prices.

# Mitsui O.S.K. Lines (9104 / MSLOF)

Our price objective of ¥6,400 is based on a multiple of 0.8x March 2026 book at the upper end of MOL's valuation range since the Red Sea crisis in 2024-25 to factor the near term boost in spot rates.

Downside risks: 1) a deterioration in macro conditions causing world trade to weaken, 2) a further increase in trade tensions and tariffs, 3) lack of capacity discipline, prolonging and deepening the period of overcapacity, and 4) a stronger Japanese yen and/or high bunker prices.

# ANA Holdings (9202 / ALNPF)

Our ANA price objective is ¥3,300 set at 1.3x March 2026 book. We set our P/B based on our FY3/28 ROE of 9.3% and the historical relationship between P/B and ROE.

Upside risks: 1) stronger inbound/outbound travel demand, 2) favorable currency or fuel price developments, and 3) improvement in shareholder return policy.

Downside risks: 1) yield deterioration due to weakening in travel demand or increased competition from other carriers, 2) sentiment impact from FX movement and higher fuel prices.

# Capcom (9697 / CCOEF)

Our PO of ¥4,700 is based on applying a 30x P/E to our FY3/27E EPS of ¥157. The applied multiple represents an approximately 30% premium to the FY25 average P/E of 23x for the 13 companies in the Game & Content cluster we cover. We apply this premium as we see the company's medium-term EPS growth for FY3/28-FY3/29 exceeding the cluster average of 7-8%.

Risks to our PO are: (1) delays in new title launches, (2) slower repeat sales of catalog titles, and (3) a deterioration in the global economy.

# Fast Retailing (9983 / FRCOF / FRCOY)

We set our PO at ¥84,000 (ADR: US\$54.19), based on c.41x FY8/28 P/E. We derived our PO with reference to the stock's 12-month forward P/E over roughly the past three months, during which the market has started to factor in signs that the China business is bottoming out, as well as the view that growth in the North America and Europe businesses will drive overall earnings.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) a turnaround in earnings in China and Southeast Asia, and (2) GU entering a new growth stage. Downside risks are: (1) yen appreciation, which would reduce the contribution of overseas businesses to consolidated results, (2) a slower-than-expected recovery in China, and (3) adverse weather and country risk

BofA is currently acting as Financial Advisor to AURELIUS in connection with its proposed disposal of LSG Asia-Pacific to a consortium comprising of Kobe Bussan Co., Ltd. and GOURMET KINEYA Co., Ltd., which was announced on March 31, 2026.

BofA is currently acting as financial advisor to Resona Holdings, Inc. in connection with the proposed sale of a 20% stake in its subsidiary, Kansai Mirai Bank, Limited, to West Japan Railway Company, which was announced on May 1, 2026.

# Disclosures

# Important Disclosures

# BofA Global Research Credit Opinion Key

BofA Global Research provides recommendations on an issuer's bonds (including corporate and sovereign external debt securities), loans, capital securities, equity preferreds and CDS as described below. Convertible securities are not rated. An issuer level recommendation may also be provided for an issuer as explained below. BofA Global Research credit recommendations are assigned using a three-month time horizon.

Issuer Recommendations: If an issuer credit recommendation is provided, it is applicable to bonds and capital securities of the issuer except bonds and capital securities specifically referenced in the report with a different credit recommendation. Where there is no issuer credit recommendation, only individual bonds and capital securities with specific recommendations are covered. Loans, CDS and equity preferreds are rated separately and issuer recommendations do not apply to them.

BofA Global Research credit recommendations are assigned using a three-month time horizon:

Overweight: Spreads and /or excess returns are likely to outperform the relevant and comparable market over the next three months.

Marketweight: Spreads and/or excess returns are likely to perform in-line with the relevant and comparable market over the next three months.

Underweight: Spreads and/or excess returns are likely to underperform the relevant and comparable market over the next three months.

BofA Global Research uses the following rating system with respect to Credit Default Swaps (CDS):

Buy Protection: Buy CDS, therefore going short credit risk.

Neutral: No purchase or sale of CDS is recommended.

Sell Protection: Sell CDS, therefore going long credit risk.

$^{R1}$ Issuers that were investment banking clients of BofA or one of its affiliates within the past 12 months. For purposes of this Investment Rating Distribution, the coverage universe includes only stocks. A stock rated Neutral is included as a Hold, and a stock rated Underperform is included as a Sell.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst's assessment of both a stock's absolute total return potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 - Buy stocks are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm's guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst's view of the potential price appreciation (depreciation).

Investment rating Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial rating) Ratings dispersion guidelines for coverage cluster $^{R2}$ 

<table><tr><td>Buy</td><td>≥ 10%</td><td>≤ 70%</td></tr><tr><td>Neutral</td><td>≥ 0%</td><td>≤ 30%</td></tr><tr><td>Underperform</td><td>N/A</td><td>≥ 20%</td></tr></table>

$^{R2}$ Ratings dispersions may vary from time to time where BofA Global Research believes it better reflects the investment prospects of stocks in a Coverage Cluster.

INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be secure) and 9 - pays no cash dividend. Coverage Cluster is comprised of stocks covered by a single analyst or two or more analysts sharing a common industry, sector, region or other classification(s). A stock's coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.
